Critical findings (4)

CRITICAL1 address(es) authenticated successfully after failing
An address that guessed wrong and then got it right is the signature of a successful credential attack. Every account named here needs its session activity reviewed and its credentials rotated.
category: Authentication  |  ATT&CK: T1110 Brute Force / T1078 Valid Accounts  |  artifact: /var/log/btmp, auth.log, journal
seen: 1 occurrence(s), 2021-12-08 16:08:20 .. 2021-12-08 16:09:12 UTC
198.18.0.0: 7 failure(s) then SUCCESS - Dec  8 16:10:13 ApacheWebServer sshd[3626]: Accepted publickey for root from 198.18.0.0 port 59883 ssh2: RSA SHA2...
CRITICAL1 executable file(s) in world-writable temp directories
/tmp, /var/tmp and /dev/shm are the default drop locations for payloads. /dev/shm is memory-backed, so files there disappear on reboot - an attacker choice, not an accident.
category: Filesystem  |  ATT&CK: T1036 Masquerading / T1059  |  artifact: bodyfile/bodyfile.txt
seen: 1 occurrence(s), 2021-11-11 19:09:51 UTC
-rwxr-xr-x /var/tmp/dk86  uid=1 size=48748     mtime=2021-11-11 19:09:51
CRITICAL1 process(es) executing from a world-writable directory
Binaries under /tmp, /var/tmp, /dev/shm or /run are the standard staging location for dropped payloads. Legitimate services do not run from these paths.
category: Process  |  ATT&CK: T1059 / T1036 Masquerading  |  artifact: live_response/process/running_processes_full_paths.txt
seen: 1 occurrence(s), 2021-12-05 08:28:02 UTC
PID 24330   daemon     /tmp/agettyd (deleted)                 agettyd -c noresetd
CRITICAL2 process(es) running a deleted binary
The on-disk file was unlinked while the process kept running - a deliberate anti-forensic pattern. The executable can still be recovered from /proc/<pid>/exe on the live host or from the memory image in this collection.
category: Process  |  ATT&CK: T1070.004 Indicator Removal: File Deletion  |  artifact: live_response/process/running_processes_full_paths.txt

High findings (45)

Medium findings (19)

MEDIUMAccount(s) present in /etc/passwd but not in the backup copy
/etc/passwd- is the previous version written on the last account change; names appearing only in the live file were added most recently. Package installs create service accounts this way too, so check the name against the software inventory before treating it as attacker activity.
category: Account  |  ATT&CK: T1136 Create Account  |  artifact: [root]/etc/passwd
seen: 1 occurrence(s)
nxautomation:997
MEDIUMMembership of privileged groups
sudo/wheel/docker/lxd/disk/shadow membership is equivalent to root on most systems - confirm every member is expected.

MEDIUMSystem account(s) with an interactive shell
Service accounts normally have nologin/false. An interactive shell on a low uid is a quiet persistence trick.

MEDIUMShell history missing or empty for interactive user(s)
An interactive account that logged in but left no history is consistent with `history -c`, HISTFILE=/dev/null, or the file being deleted. It can also simply mean the shell has not exited yet - correlate with the login records.
